Teeka 7— Arthasangraha Teeka - Raagavendra

Select Teeka
Arthasangraha Teeka - Raagavendra

Teeka Entry
Sanskrit Text (Devanagari)








रा० टी०-भगवन्माहात्म्यज्ञानतद्भक्तिपूर्वकभगवदाराधनरूपतया स्वविहितवृत्तित्वेन वाऽनुष्ठीयमानो ना-

रायणद्विट्तदनुबन्धिनिग्रहः क्षत्रियाणां परमो धर्मः । तं धर्म बन्धुस्नेहाद्धर्मत्वेनोक्तदिशा शङ्कमानमर्जुनं प्र-

त्युक्तरूपो निग्रह एव परमधर्मः तद्विरुद्धः सर्वोपि अधर्म इति कृत्स्नगीताशास्त्रेण बोधयिष्यन्नादौ तावद्वि-

वेकेन तव शोक इत्याह-अशोच्यानिति। अशोच्यान् शोकानर्हान् हे पार्थ! त्वं अन्वशाचेः। यांस्त्वं शोचितवानभूस्ते

न शोकार्हा इति यावत् । प्रज्ञावादांश्च भाषसे । प्रज्ञयोत्था वादाः प्रज्ञावादाः स्वबुद्धिमात्रपरिकल्पिता वादाः

तान्भाषसे यान्प्रभाषसे ते स्वबुद्धिमात्रपरिकल्पिता वादाः न वृद्धसंमता इति यावत् । यद्वा प्रकर्षेण जा-

मन्तीति प्रज्ञाः । “ इगुपधज्ञाप्रीकिरः कः ” तेषामवादाः प्रज्ञावादाः प्राज्ञविरुद्धवादास्तान्प्रभाषस इत्यर्थः ।

नहि दृष्ट्वेमं स्वजनं कृष्णेत्याद्यर्जुनवाक्येषु कश्चित्प्राज्ञसंमतवादोस्ति । नहि प्राज्ञा नारायणद्विटूतदनुबन्धि-

निग्रहमधर्म वदन्तीतिभावः । ननु आसन्नविनाशास्ते कथमशोच्या इत्यतआह-गतासूनिति । पण्डिताः

ज्ञानिनः गतासूनासन्नविनाशान् अगतासूनिवेत्युपमार्थश्चकारः । जीवतइव तान्नानुशोचन्ति यतोऽतो न शो-

च्यास्त इति तानन्वशोचस्त्वमित्यर्थः ॥ ११ ॥



English Translation








The Supreme Duty of a Warrior: The destruction of those who hate Lord Narayana and their associates is the supreme duty (parama dharma) of the Kshatriyas (warriors). This is true whether it is performed as an act of worship to the Lord—preceded by knowledge of His greatness and devotion—or simply as the performance of one's own prescribed professional duty.

The Context of the Gita: Arjuna, due to affection for his kinsmen, doubted this duty and questioned whether it was truly "dharma." To address this, the entire Gita scripture intends to teach that such destruction (of the wicked) is indeed the highest dharma, and anything contrary to it is adharma (unrighteousness). To begin this instruction, the Lord first points out that Arjuna’s grief is a result of a lack of discrimination (viveka), saying: "Asocyan..."

Analysis of the Verse:

"Asocyan" (Those not to be grieved for): O Partha! You have grieved for those who are unworthy of grief. In other words, those you are mourning do not deserve your sorrow.

"Prajnavadamscha bhashase" (You speak words of wisdom): Here, "Prajnavada" refers to arguments arising from your own intellect (prajna). You are speaking arguments fabricated merely by your own limited reasoning. These views are not accepted by the wise elders.

Alternative Interpretation: Alternatively, the word Prajna can mean "those who know exceptionally well" (the truly wise). Thus, Prajnavada means "non-arguments of the wise," or arguments that contradict the wise. You are uttering words that go against the conclusions of the enlightened.

The Flaw in Arjuna's Logic: In the previous statements of Arjuna (such as "O Krishna, seeing these kinsmen..."), there isn't a single argument that would be accepted by a truly wise person. This is because the wise never label the punishment of the enemies of Narayana (and their followers) as "unrighteous."

On Life and Death:

The Objection: One might ask, "But since their death is imminent, how can they be 'unworthy of grief'?"

The Answer ("Gatasun..."): The Lord says that Panditah (the truly enlightened) do not grieve for those whose breath has departed (gatasun) or for those whose death is imminent, just as they do not grieve for those whose breath has not yet departed (agatasun).

Conclusion: Since the wise treat the living and the dying with the same equanimity, those men are not to be mourned. Therefore, the implication is that you (Arjuna) have been grieving in vain.

Teeka 8— Bhaavaprakasha -Sadhaanandi Teeka - Sadanandha

Select Teeka
Bhaavaprakasha -Sadhaanandi Teeka - Sadanandha

Teeka Entry
Sanskrit Text (Devanagari)








स० टी०-तत्रार्जुनस्य संग्रामे प्रवृत्तिरभवत्स्वनः । मोहेन द्विविधेनैषा प्रतिबद्धा स च द्विधा ॥ १ ॥ मो-

हआदौ निराकार्यस्तत्स्वरूपं प्रदर्श्यते । स्वप्रकाशे परानन्दे शुद्धेऽसंसारिणि स्वतः ॥ २ ॥ शरीरत्रितयाध्या-

सात्संसारे सत्यता मतिः । अनात्मनि शरीरादावात्मताधीर्भमात्मिका ॥ ३ ॥ सर्वसाधारणो मोह एकोऽसा-

धारणोऽपरः । स्वधर्मेऽधर्मताबुद्धिर्युद्धे पार्थस्य याऽभवत् ॥ ४ ॥ मोहोऽयमपरोऽप्यत्रासाधारणपदाभिधः ।

देहत्रयाद्विविच्यामतत्त्वं प्रत्यञ्चमागमैः ॥ ५ ॥ यो बोधः स्यात्स आद्यस्य मोहस्य विनिवर्तकः । स्वधर्म-

त्वेन युद्धस्य हिसादीनां न दोषता ॥ ६ ॥ एवं बोधो द्वितीयस्य संमोहस्य निवर्तकः । हेतुनाशे न शोकस्य

विनिवृत्तिः स्वतो भवेत् ॥ ७ ॥ इत्यभिप्रेत्य भगवांस्तद्वयं क्रमशो वदन् । उवाच सर्वशोकाद्यध्यासमूलं

निवर्तयन् ॥ ८॥ भीष्मादीनात्मसहितानयोग्यानेव शोचितुम्। पण्डितः सन्नहो तांस्त्वमनुशोचितवानसि ॥९॥

म्रियन्ते मन्निमित्तं ते कि करिष्यामि तैर्विना । कि नो राज्यादिनेत्यादिवचोभिर्बहुधा भ्रमान् ॥ १० ॥

शोच्यभ्रमस्तवाशोच्ये स मान्यस्तिर्यगादिभिः । अत्यन्तपण्डितस्यायं नोचितः सर्वथा भवेत् ॥ ११ ॥ प्राज्ञै-

र्वक्तुमयोग्यांश्च शब्दान्वदसि नैकधा । कथं भीष्ममहं संख्ये इत्यादीस्त्वं न लज्जया ॥ १२ ॥ तूष्णीं भव-

सि कि ते स्यादनौचित्यमतःपरम् । यद्वा प्रज्ञावतांवादान्भाषसे न तु बुद्धयसे ॥ १३ ॥ मौढ्यं पाण्डित्य-

मेकत्र त्वयि दृष्टं तद्भुतम् । ननु बन्धुवियोगेन शोकः स्याद्धीमतामपि ॥ १४ ॥ मैवं धीमत्त्वमेतद्भोः प्रहा-

सायैव कल्पते । ये, पण्डिता गुरोः श्रुत्वा वेदान्तविषयं पदम् ॥ १५ ॥ ब्रह्मैक्यं युक्तिभिर्मत्वा निदिध्यास्य

निरन्तरम् । साक्षात्कृतात्मतत्त्वास्ते नष्टाविद्यामलाबुधाः ॥ १६ ॥ मृतान्वा जीवतो बन्धून्नानुशोचन्ति कल्पि-

तान् । एते सर्वे परित्यज्य गतास्तिष्ठन्ति कुत्र ते ॥ १७ ॥ जीविष्यन्ति कथं चैते जीवन्तो बन्धवो मम ।

बन्धुविच्छेदतश्चैवं न व्यामुह्यन्ति पण्डिताः ॥ १८ ॥ यथा स्वप्ने मृतो बन्धुर्जीवन्वा शोच्यतां गतः । न

तन्निमित्तको मोहो जागरेऽप्यनुवर्तते ॥१९॥ एवमज्ञानजभ्रान्त्या कल्पिता बन्धवो मृताः । जीवन्तो वा न ते

बोधे शोकमोहप्रदाः सताम् ॥ २० ॥ देहादेर्नश्वरात्मत्वान्नित्यत्वात्प्रत्यगात्मनः । न शोच्याः प्राणिनः सर्वे

स्वात्मतत्त्वविदां सताम् ॥ २१ ॥ एवं त्वं पण्डितो भूत्वा माकार्षी. शोकमान्ध्यजम् । स्वात्मतत्त्वे सदा

स्थैर्य प्राप्नुहीत्याशयो हरेः ॥ २२ ॥ ११ ॥



English Translation








This text is the Siddhanta-Deepika (Sa. Ti.), another classical commentary on the Bhagavad Gita (2.11). It provides a more philosophical and structural analysis of Arjuna's delusion and how Krishna’s teaching addresses it.

Siddhanta-Deepika Commentary: English Translation

The Twofold Nature of Delusion: Arjuna had a natural inclination to fight in the war, but this was obstructed by a twofold delusion (moha). The Lord first intends to remove this delusion, the nature of which is now explained:

General Delusion (Ordinary): The Self is self-luminous, supreme bliss, pure, and inherently untouched by the world. However, by falsely superimposing the three bodies (gross, subtle, and causal) upon the Self, one develops the notion that the world is real. This creates the erroneous belief that the non-self (the body, etc.) is the Self.

Specific Delusion (Extraordinary): This is the specific confusion Arjuna faced—thinking that his own duty (svadharma), the war, was actually unrighteous (adharma).

The Remedy for Delusion:

The first delusion is removed by the knowledge of the "Inmost Self" (Pratyag-atman) as distinct from the three bodies, gained through the Vedas and scriptures.

The second delusion is removed by realizing that since war is a prescribed duty (svadharma), the violence involved does not constitute a sin.

When the cause (ignorance/delusion) is destroyed, grief automatically disappears. With this intent, the Lord begins to remove the root of all grief and superimposition.

Explanation of Verse 11:

The Irony of Arjuna’s Grief: O Arjuna! You are grieving for Bhishma and others, including yourself, who are fundamentally unworthy of grief. While acting as if you are a wise man (pandita), you have actually fallen into sorrow. You speak words like, "They are dying because of me," "What will I do without them?" and "What use is the kingdom?" These are various forms of delusion.

The Critique of "Wise Words": You harbor the delusion of "grieve-worthiness" regarding those who are not to be grieved for. Such a delusion is found even in animals; it is entirely inappropriate for one who claims to be a scholar. You utter words that the truly wise would never say—such as, "How shall I attack Bhishma?" Do you not feel shame? What could be more inappropriate than this? Or perhaps, you repeat the arguments of the wise (prajnavada) without actually understanding their meaning.

True Wisdom vs. Ignorance: It is a great wonder to see both extreme foolishness and a pretense of scholarship in you at the same time! If you argue that even wise men feel grief at the loss of kinsmen, that is incorrect. Such "wisdom" is only fit for mockery.

The Vision of the Realized Sages (Panditah): Those who are truly wise have:

Heard the Vedantic teachings from a Guru.

Contemplated the oneness of Brahman through logic.

Meditated continuously and realized the Truth of the Self.

Having destroyed the impurity of ignorance, they do not grieve for kinsmen—whether dead or living—because they recognize them as mental projections (kalpita). They do not worry, "Where have they gone?" or "How will my living kinsmen survive without me?"

The Analogy of the Dream: Just as one might grieve for a dead or living relative in a dream, but that delusion does not persist once the person wakes up, similarly, kinsmen imagined through the error of ignorance do not cause grief or delusion to the "Awakened ones" (Sataam).

Final Conclusion: Because the body is perishable and the inner Self is eternal, no living being is worthy of grief in the eyes of those who know the Truth. Therefore, the Lord’s intent is: "Do not act out of the blindness of grief; instead, attain constant steadiness in the truth of your own Self."
